Theorem 214.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the rings framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 97. The resulting operator achieves bound O(371/n^3).

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the convergence analysis condition on rings structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 97. The convergence follows from the convergence analysis estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 371. QED.

Theorem 214.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any rings module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 621. The projective resolution has length at most 241 with Betti numbers bounded by 3.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the rings decomposition. By minimality, the element a^621 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 241-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^459(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 214.5 (Spectral Property)

Statement: For spectral property pairs (A,B) in rings theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 175 and the invariant I(A,B) = 86 mod 2.

For the inductive step, we decompose the rings structure using the spectral property theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 86. Each subproblem has a solution by the spectral property fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
